I don’t really notice how much until after.\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I am. If the scale goes up even a little, or my work apron feels tight around my waist, I kind of panic. It feels like I’ve ruined everything… like I’m just going to get fat right away.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape is different from what others see when they look at you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. Like, I can be fine at work, doing my job, and then I catch myself in the glass and my stomach or hips look… too big. Then I just feel like I failed somehow.\n\nDoctor: How often do you eat an amount of food that feels excessive to you in a short period of time?\n\nPatient: Most weeks… it happens at least a couple times. It’s been like that for months, I guess. I eat way more than I meant to in a short time, and then I try to make up for it somehow.\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up the main way you try to make up for eating too much?\n\nPatient: Yeah… mostly. I make myself throw up after I eat too much. Sometimes at work, in the bathroom, or as soon as I get home. I don’t like saying that, but it’s… kind of the only thing that makes me feel like I can calm down after.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you eat an amount of food that feels excessive to you?\n\nPatient: Usually a couple times a week… sometimes more. It’s been going on for months.\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up the only way you try to make up for eating too much, or do you also use other methods like laxatives or excessive exercise?\n\nPatient: It’s not only that. I don’t really use laxatives, but… the day after, I’ll try not to eat until really late. Or I’ll make myself walk or exercise, even if my legs are already hurting from standing all day at the cleaners.\n\nDoctor: Is making yourself throw up the only way you try to compensate for eating too much?\n\nPatient: No… it’s not the only way. The next day I’ll usually try not to eat till late, or I’ll make myself walk or exercise, even when my legs are already hurting from being on my feet at the cleaners.\n\nDoctor: When you eat an amount of food that feels excessive to you, do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… once I start, it’s like I can’t make myself stop. I’ll be telling myself to quit, but my hands just keep grabbing more food. It feels like I’m kind of watching it happen.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

The next day I’ll usually try not to eat till late, or I’ll make myself walk or exercise, even when my legs are already hurting from being on my feet at the cleaners.\n\nDoctor: When you eat an amount of food that feels excessive to you, do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started?\n\nPatient: Yeah… once I start, it’s like I can’t make myself stop. I’ll be telling myself to quit, but my hands just keep grabbing more food. It feels like I’m kind of watching it happen.\n\nDoctor: How many times a month do you make yourself throw up after eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t know exactly… but it’s a lot. Most weeks it happens several times, sometimes after I eat and panic. Maybe… more than twenty times a month.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do you have episodes where you eat an excessive amount of food and feel like you can't stop?\n\nPatient: Most weeks… at least a couple times. It’s been going on for months. I eat way more than I meant to and then I just… panic and try to undo it.\n\nDoctor: Do you have episodes of excessive eating almost every day?\n\nPatient: Not every day… but when it does happen, it’s a lot. Like I’ll go through chips, pastries, leftovers, sweet drinks… all in one sitting, and then I realize how much is gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up after almost every time you eat an excessive amount of food?\n\nPatient: Yeah… most times. If I eat too much, I usually end up making myself throw up—sometimes at work, sometimes as soon as I get home. It’s… it feels like the only way I can calm down after.\n\nDoctor: Do you have episodes of eating an excessive amount of food at least once a week?\n\nPatient: Yes… most weeks it’s at least once. Usually more like a couple times, for months now. Then I try to make up for it somehow… like throwing up or not eating for a while.\n\nDoctor: Do you have episodes of eating an excessive amount of food at least three times a week?\n\nPatient: Yeah… sometimes it’s that often. When it happens, I can go through a whole bag of chips, pastries, leftovers, and sweet drinks in one sitting before I really notice.\n\nDoctor: Do you have episodes of eating an excessive amount of food almost every day?\n\nPatient: It’s… close to that sometimes. I don’t know if I’d say every single day, but when it happens, I can go through chips, pastries, leftovers, sweet drinks… a lot, before I really notice.\n\nDoctor: Do you make yourself throw up after almost every time you eat?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a lot of the time. Not literally every single time, but after I eat I get this panic, and I’ll make myself throw up… sometimes at work in the bathroom, or as soon as I get home. It feels like the only way I can settle down.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
